### **OVERALL STRUCTURE OF THE STUDY TEAM** 

Dr. Knobel has assembled a team of experts in exposure science, exposomics, machine learning, and aging that brings the complementary expertise this proposal requires.Dr. Knobel (PI) is a geospatial epidemiologist whose work focuses on urban environmental exposures and aging-related outcomes, bridging exposure science and environmental epidemiology through geospatial methods. Dr. Yitshak-Sade (Co-I) is an environmental epidemiologist specializing in the health effects of environmental mixtures among older adults, with extensive experience working with large longitudinal cohort data. Dr. Kloog (Co-I) is an exposure scientist and GIS specialist with expertise in developing spatiotemporal exposure datasets, machine learning for prediction, and managing large multi-layer spatial data. Dr. Midya (Co-I) is a biostatistician specializing in mixture methods and machine learning for epidemiological applications. Dr. Federman (Co-I) is an internist whose research focuses on the use of electronic health records to improve the identification and management of cognitive impairment in older adults. Dr. Wolf (Co-I) is a cognitive and behavioral scientist and health services researcher focused on aging. Dr. Rogers (Co-I) is a biostatistician with expertise in aging cohorts and biological aging.

### **CONSORTIUM AGREEMENT** 

The appropriate programmatic and administrative personnel of each institution involved in this grant application are aware of all applicable Federal regulations and policies and will establish the necessary inter-institutional agreement(s) consistent with those policies. 

### **Northwestern University** 

Dr. Wolf is a cognitive/behavioral scientist and health services researcher with a focus on aging. He is Director of the Center for Applied Health Research on Aging and Associate Vice-Chair of the Department of Medicine at Northwestern University’s Feinberg School of Medicine. He is the Principal Investigator of the Health Literacy and Cognitive Function Among Older Adults (LitCog) cohort, the longitudinal study at the core of Aim 2 of this proposal. Dr. Wolf will provide scientific support for all LitCog-related analyses, advise on the interpretation of findings across the domains of cognitive function, health literacy, and aging, and contribute to manuscript editing. 

Dr. Rogers is a biostatistician with expertise in aging cohorts and biological aging. She is the lead data analyst of the LitCog cohort and has extensive experience with the study’s longitudinal data structure, outcome measures, and analytic workflows. Dr. Rogers will support the statistical analyses for Aim 2, including data preparation, and will contribute to Aim 3 analyses using the LitCog data. She will also participate in manuscript editing. 

The Mount Sinai and Northwestern teams will meet yearly, and as needed.

## May 7, 2026 

## Dear Dr. Knobel, 

I write in strong support of your R01 application, “The Urban Exposome and Healthy Aging: From Evidence to Intervention Projections Across Two US Cities”. I will serve as Site Principal Investigator for the Northwestern University component of this project and to contribute the LitCog cohort and my team’s expertise in healthy aging and longitudinal cohort methods. 

My research program focuses on the cognitive and functional consequences of aging in community-dwelling older adults, with particular attention to how social and environmental context shapes cognitive trajectories over time. I bring specific expertise in longitudinal cohort study design, cognitive assessment, and the analysis of repeated-measures data in aging populations. Dr. Lauren Opsasnick (Co-Investigator) will contribute complementary measurement and research methodology expertise, and together we will support the study design, analysis, and interpretation of findings. Ms. Bonham (Project Manager) and Ms. Yeh (Data Analyst) will facilitate data management and sharing from LitCog. 

This proposal addresses a question of real public health importance: how do the built, social, and ambient features of urban environments shape the pace of aging across biological, cognitive, and functional domains? Existing work has examined individual exposures in isolation, but few studies have applied an exposome framework to healthy aging outcomes. Your team’s approach, combining large-scale electronic health record data from New York City with the rich neuropsychological and functional assessments available in LitCog, is exactly the kind of complementary design needed to move this field forward. 

The scientific questions motivating this proposal align closely with the long-term goals of the LitCog study, and I am confident that combining our two cohorts—one optimized for scale and clinical depth, the other for longitudinal neuropsychological and functional detail—will yield findings that neither dataset could produce alone. I look forward to working closely with you and your team throughout the project. 

Please do not hesitate to contact me if reviewers or program officers have any questions about the Northwestern component of this work. 

Sincerely, 



Michael Wolf, PhD MPH MA James R. Webster, Jr. Professor of Medicine Director, Center for Applied Health Research on Aging (CAHRA) Associate Vice Chair of Research, Department of Medicine Associate Division Chief, General Internal Medicine Feinberg School of Medicine Northwestern University
